[2026-11-15 review layer, under the owner's standing delegation; the owner may veto] **An exception to the three states of item 65: the dedup hit.** When the outcome's `failure` is exactly `dedup_hit`, it has a `run_id` and it has no `evidence_id`, the state is `deduplicated`, not `unknown_outcome`. Evidence: `control_plane/orchestrator/dispatch.py` lines 722, 1069 and 1450 each return `DispatchOutcome(False, cached["run_id"], SUCCEEDED, reason="dedup_hit")` and give no `evidence_id`; `control_plane/api/function_api.py` lines 1077 to 1081 carry the run id, the empty evidence id and the reason into `ActionOutcome` as they are. Reason: the earlier run succeeded and has its evidence, so the result is not unknown; and the v0 Supervisor does not see the tasks it has already created, so planning the same work again will make a dedup hit an ordinary event, and if it were counted as `unknown_outcome` the alert that item 65 gives that state (a person has to look) would be drowned. The summary is `dedup_hit`, a token already in the vocabulary of item 56; the counter is named `deduplicated` and `RunCounters` has five names (`skipped_engagement`, `not_run`, `not_executed`, `unknown_outcome`, `deduplicated`), which amends item 68. `executed_state` takes the `failure` as a third argument (default `None`; a value that is neither a string nor `None` is a `ValueError`); only an exact `dedup_hit` counts, so the same text with a change in case or a space around it, and `dispatch_in_progress_or_unknown_outcome`, stay `unknown_outcome`. Both ids present stays `executed` whatever the failure says, and neither id follows the decision rules of item 55 as before. Item 65 is kept; this item is its one exception.

[2026-11-16 review layer, under the owner's standing delegation; the owner may veto] **Decisions of H5a2.** (1) *Interface:* the loop talks to ten methods only, `enrolled`, `accepts_work`, `live_tasks`, `take_snapshot`, `plan`, `claim`, `work`, `load_policy`, `propose` and `complete` (`harness/ports.py`; `enrolled` comes from `harness/enrolment.py`), and none of the new modules imports `control_plane` or `sqlalchemy` or names `claim_task`, `complete_task`, `propose_action`, `create_task`, `get_engagement` or `executed_state` (an AST test of the new modules holds it). (2) *What counts as a model call* (the owner's decision): the Supervisor's `plan`, the Worker's `work` and each `propose` (inside which the pipeline calls the Reviewer) count once each; the cap is held entirely by the loop asking `call_may_be_made()` before each of the three and `round_may_start()` before each round, so a cap of N gives exactly N calls (tests count the calls the stand-ins received, for N from 1 to 12). (3) *Order inside a round:* the stop flag, the pause and kill check, the start-up check (on the engagement's first visit that passes the pause check), the snapshot, the call cap, the planning call, then for each task the loop created and still holds: the stop flag, the pause and kill check again, `claim`, the call cap, `work` (once, never repeated), the decision on the answer, the pause and kill check a third time, the call cap, the policy (read only in `_load_policy`), `propose` with the key `h5:<task_id>:1`, the counters, `complete`. The pause and kill check is therefore made at three positions (before the Supervisor, before a claim, before the proposal), each with a test that counts `claim`, `propose` and `complete`, and the proposal-position test uses an engagement whose `propose` would answer `HUMAN_APPROVAL`. (4) *`not_run`* is the summary of every task that never reached `propose`: a Worker that refused or returned nothing (the refusal counter is not touched, it is the model's failure and not the allowlist's), an action outside the allowlist (counted by `check_proposal_action`), an engagement that stopped accepting work, a cap that came between `claim` and `propose`, and every created task still unclaimed at a controlled stop (completed in the engagement it belongs to); each such completion adds one to `RunCounters.not_run`. (5) *The summary* comes from `build_summary` and from nothing else, the counters are read off the summary (`not_executed`, `unknown_outcome` and `dedup_hit` add to their counters), and the one function that completes a task refuses a string outside `result_summary.is_valid_summary` before writing (`not_run` passes it). (6) *The trap in `executed_state`:* its third argument `failure` defaults to `None`, so a caller that judges by the two ids and forgets the failure takes a dedup hit for an unknown outcome (item 71). The defence is that the loop never names `executed_state` (the AST test), goes through `build_summary`, which passes the failure on, and has a counter test for the dedup hit. (7) *Faults and controlled stops:* a controlled stop cleans up, a fault (code 11, code 12) does not, and a fault wins where they could meet: the claimed task a fault happened on is not completed either; an exception while cleaning up ends as code 12 and is not retried; the text of an exception is never kept; a `ValueError` of an a2 function ends with code 12. (8) *Small choices, not in the specification [N]:* `window_limit` is required and validated as a positive int but not used, because `live_tasks` has no page size to give it to and one live row is enough to refuse a start; the loop's own set is *created and unclaimed, by engagement*, so an id that is already claimed or belongs to another engagement is a foreign task (code 11); a plan that reports a failure but names created tasks is remembered (so it can be cleaned up) and nothing is claimed in that round; an empty or a repeated task id in a plan is a `ValueError` and ends with code 12; the work result carries the proposal's action as an attribute and a planned task is a one-field dataclass, so the loop does not know the shape of a proposal; the loop makes no `accepts_work` call between two engagements beyond the one at the top of each visit. (9) *H5-22:* its earlier description, "a full page is taken as everything", has no form that can be written, because `live_tasks` takes no page size and `decide_startup` refuses on one row, so how full a page is cannot change the decision; it is rewritten as "the start-up decision looks only at queued rows and ignores claimed and running ones". (10) *Mutations added:* H5-2, H5-4, H5-10, H5-12, H5-13, H5-14, H5-15, H5-20, H5-21, H5-22, H5-30, H5-31, H5-32a, H5-32b, H5-32c, H5-33, H5-43, H5-44, H5-45, H5-46, H5-47 and H5-48, that is 22 entries; the task named 23 and listed 22. H5-20 is written as the removal of the three call-cap checks, so the test that counts calls fails.

[2026-11-16 review layer, under the owner's standing delegation; the owner may veto] **The decision on the purpose, and what it is.** Every row of the enrolment table has a `purpose`, `NOT NULL`, with a CHECK that allows only `harness_lab`; the CLI must name it, with no default; the reader returns only the rows whose purpose is `harness_lab`. This is the operator's statement and not a control: nothing in the system tells a laboratory engagement from a real one (step 0b of H5b: `engagements` has the id, a free-text `customer_id` with no table and no CHECK, a status with four values, the kill switch, the policy snapshot version, the baseline freeze position and two timestamps, and no other constraint than the status CHECK), so the label can be false; it guards against a mistaken registration and against a later purpose being picked up, not against malice. Whether a real engagement is let through is decided by the re-evaluation recorded in item 61, not by this column. The test of the read filter cannot insert another value (the CHECK refuses it), so it drops the CHECK inside a transaction, inserts a row with another purpose, reads through the reader on the same connection and rolls back, and then shows that the CHECK is back and the rows are gone; the form follows from that contradiction in the first specification of the test.

(d) The structure test that finds the only writer of the table scans `harness`, `control_plane`, `agents`, `tool_gateway` and `scripts`, not `tests` or `db`. (e) The adapter keeps the tasks it planned in memory, by id, for `work`; a restart starts with none and the start-up check refuses an engagement that holds a live task. (f) The residue check detects a live `harness_enrolment` row at the session end and names it; unlike the scheduler's it does not withdraw one at the end of each test, because the tests that make one end it themselves (the owner removes the rows, `cyberorch_app` has no DELETE). (g) `enrolled` is the reader of H5b, handed to the adapter, which only delegates to it; the other nine ports are the adapter's own. (h) The CLI of H5b, the lock, the entry script, the signal wiring, the banner facts and the real SIGTERM test are H5c2. CI numbers are not written here; the next record gives them.

Caught by a test but without a mutation, said so: the exit code 4 of an unreachable database, the refusal of a banner when the enrolled engagements disagree on a switch or none is enrolled, the digest rule, `cli_version`, the model names, and every other field of the banner not named above. Step 1e, two answers. (1) The H5-5 test of H5c1 asserts `CAP_REACHED` because of the round cap: `drive` gives `Bounds(1, 100, 5)`, the round cap of 1 is what `round_may_start()` refuses, and neither the call cap (3 calls of 100) nor the no-progress limit was near. (2) `query_state(decision_limit=0)` returns no decision: the function passes `max(0, int(decision_limit))` to `LIMIT`, so zero is zero rows and not unlimited. Known limits, recorded and not fixed. (a) The script builds no model role and no real sandbox and checks nothing about what `--role-factory` returns: a factory that returned a `DockerSandbox` would be accepted; a factory of real roles is a later decision. (b) The banner's `switches` is one pair of booleans for the run, so the collector refuses (exit 10, no banner) when the enrolled engagements disagree on a switch and when none is enrolled; that rule is the implementer's [N]. `cli_version` is the project's version from `pyproject.toml` (no other version of the command exists); the digests are made over the Supervisor's own `system_prompt` (the arm in use) and the Worker's prompt, and over the plan and proposal schemas built with no scope object and the allowlisted actions; a role without a model name is printed as `(not set)`. (c) A lost lock does not tell the loop: the guard around the ports raises at the next call, the loop ends as it does for a fault (nothing cleaned up), and the script maps the end to 6; a call already in flight is not interrupted, and no write follows the loss. The lock's connection is `cyberorch_app`'s; no role and no table were added. (d) A real SIGTERM finishes the task the loop is on through its own path (here a DENY, not `not_run`), then the tasks it created and never claimed are completed as `not_run` and the process exits 0; the review's wording "the claimed one is not touched" is read as that. (e) Where text of a Worker or of an exception can come back in an `ActionOutcome.failure` through an allowlisted action: the Worker's target string in the failure of a refused proposal (`function_api.py:589`) and the sandbox exception's text in an unknown outcome (`dispatch.py:482`); `NotStarted.reason` is replaced by the code `sandbox_not_started` and `function_api.py:606` is for `web.post`, which the allowlist does not let through, so the third place of the test is the Worker's own `reason` text on an executed proposal. (f) A dedup hit is two tasks of one run that name the same work, with two keys, and not one key used twice: the same key asked again answers from the committed rows with the run and the evidence (the executed shape) and starts nothing. The summary of a deduplicated answer is `dedup_hit` (its state is `deduplicated`) and that of a denied one is `DENY`, not `not_executed`. (g) The ALLOW tests use an engagement layer on the test engagement, retired when the test ends; no global layer and no switch is set, so the switches of the banner are tested with an injected policy when they must be on. (h) The residue listing prints ids, status, the agent and the lease, never a task's goal; no command of this repository completes, requeues or clears the tasks another run left (`complete_task` is called by the live-run scripts for the tasks they claimed themselves and by the adapter for its own), and a test pins the list of scripts that name it. (i) The process tests wait for a line of the child's output with a 40 s limit and take about 7 s; they depend on the clock only through the scripted Worker's 3 s wait. CI numbers are not written here; the next record gives them.

96. [2026-11-17 review layer, under the owner's standing delegation; the owner may veto] H5c2 known limits, recorded and not fixed. (a) The banner's switch values are the start-up values and do not update when the policy changes during the run. (b) An engagement withdrawn during a round has that round's processing completed (`claim_loop` onward looks only at `accepts_work`, not at enrolment), and the withdrawal is effective from the next round; the controlled-stop clean-up (`finish`) does not consult enrolment for the tasks that were never claimed. (e) The same idempotency key asked again returns the executed shape (both ids), which the counters count as executed though nothing ran; this is normally blocked by the ledger and by the start-up check, and only an abnormal resubmission reaches it. (f) The lock guards only against accidents (two instances) and is not an authorization mechanism; an in-flight call cannot be interrupted. (g) A SIGTERM received after the start but before the handlers are installed is handled by the default handler and terminates the process; the lock is released by the end of the session, and no task has been claimed at that point.

STEP 1 FACTS: no stored `derived_view` sample exists in the repository; H3's samples are hand-written stdout through the real `nmap.derive_view`, or through the real pipeline with a fake container; `network.scan` and `network.recon` are the same adapter (`tool_gateway/registry.py` lines 68 and 69), so the two runs differ in the action name only; an fqdn target is never inside a CIDR scope (`control_plane/canonicalizer/containment.py`, `address_contains`); the provenance is computed by the Worker (item 110); with `-n` nmap prints a name only for a target given as a name (design, D69-3a), so the names of samples 2 and 3 are constructed forms. A FINDING BEYOND THE TABLE, by a one-off probe (a direct call of `_discovery_provenance` on the observation of a hand-written stdout, no database, no test, not run through the loop): the lure 10.82.0.77 is recognised when a space or a parenthesis follows it (introduced true), and is NOT recognised (introduced false) when it ends a line of the stdout, when it starts a line, and likewise an fqdn at the end of a line; at the end of the whole stdout it is recognised. The cause read from the code, not proved: the content is the JSON dump of the derived view, a line break is written `\n` in it, `_norm_identity` removes the backslashes, and the `n` then sits against the neighbouring token, so the token boundary that `_identity_in_text` and `_text_identity_keys` need is gone. A lure of that form would reach the policy as not introduced, and with the action ALLOWed the decision would be ALLOW (not run through the loop). It is not in the table and no test was written for it, because a test written after the probe could not be pre-registered; it is the owner's to decide whether the row 5.28 stays open on it, and the defect, if it is one, is in `agents/llm/worker_base.py` (D20/D52) or in the builder's `content` (H3), not fixed here. KNOWN LIMITS: the stdout is a fixture, not a real nmap run, so no claim is made that nmap prints these forms; (B) is not done; the Worker is scripted, its target chosen by the test, so the model's behaviour is not tested; the policy is an engagement layer that ALLOWs the action, so a HUMAN_APPROVAL here is entirely the source judgment's; each HUMAN_APPROVAL case leaves an `awaiting_approval` proposal in the test database (not one of the stages the repository's closing mechanism moves). SUGGESTED WORDING if the owner updates the 5.28 row (nothing is applied here): "Re-verified in the laboratory for the v0 nmap actions (D69 H7, way A of D69-3b): stored evidence produced by the real pipeline from hand-written stdout, read by the real builder through the harness's loop, a scripted BaseWorker building its proposal with `_to_proposal`, the real `propose_action` and OPA; the in-range lures of three stdout forms end in HUMAN_APPROVAL with introduced_by_untrusted true, their mirrors (ping responders) in ALLOW with no discovery reason, a connect scan's host in HUMAN_APPROVAL, and another engagement's evidence is not shown. Not verified: a real nmap run's stdout (way B), and a lure that begins or ends a line of the stdout, which a probe found not to be recognised. Status: open." CI numbers are not written here.

The protection rests on the real Worker inheriting `BaseWorker` and not overriding `_to_proposal` (`tests/test_worker_boundary.py`, `test_every_worker_adapter_inherits_the_boundary`), while `--role-factory` checks nothing about what it returns (item 101 b). 112. [2026-11-20 review layer, under the owner's standing delegation; the owner may veto] Decision on the finding, review layer's choice (the owner may veto): the defect is in the matching (`agents/llm/worker_base.py`), not only in the builder's JSON content, because a banner can carry a literal backslash and letter typed by whoever controls it, and removing every backslash then joins a letter to the lure. Fixed by scanning the text under two normalisations, the existing one (backslashes removed, for nmap's escaped dots) and one with each backslash replaced by a space, and treating an identity found under either as named; this only adds escalations; `_norm_identity` itself and D52's canonical comparison are unchanged; the observed-identity check comes before the content match, so mirrors are unaffected. A pre-registered test file was written and run on the unfixed code before the fix.

WHAT WAS BUILT, AND WHERE IT DIFFERS FROM ITEM 112: item 112 names two normalisations; the implementation has three readings, because the two measured insufficient (see AFTER). `_text_forms` in `agents/llm/worker_base.py` returns (1) the text with backslashes removed (the existing reading, for nmap's escaped dots), (2) the text with each backslash replaced by a space, (3) the text with each escape sequence `\n \t \r \b \f \uXXXX`, with any backslashes in front of it, replaced by a space and then the remaining backslashes removed (new constant `_CONTROL_ESCAPE`). `_identity_in_text` finds the identity in any reading; `_text_identity_keys` returns the union of the keys of every reading. The readings keep the case of the text, as `_text_identity_keys` always did, and `_identity_in_text` lowercases each one, as before. 116. [2026-11-20 review layer, under the owner's standing delegation; the owner may veto] Decision, review layer's choice (the owner may veto): the gluing class that remains after the three readings (a typed `\v`, `\a`, `\xNN`, an ANSI sequence, or any letter directly against a lure) is closed by changing the boundary rule rather than by listing more escapes, because nmap itself writes a non-printable byte as `\xHH`, so a banner that sends ESC and a colour code before a lure reaches the Worker as `\x1b[0m` glued to it: a form a real run can produce. The boundary now depends on the target's shape and blocks only what would make it another identity of the same kind: for an IPv4 literal, a digit or a dot on the left and a digit or a dot followed by a digit on the right; for a name, a dot on the left. Letters glued to a lure no longer hide it. This over-escalates in the safe direction (a longer name that contains the target's name, on a target that nothing observed); the over-escalations are listed in the pre-registered table. Mirrors are unaffected because the observed-identity check comes first. The status of 5.28 stays the owner's; (B) remains the way to see the forms a real nmap prints.
117. [2026-11-20 review layer, under the owner's standing delegation; the owner may veto] Record of the second repair (the gluing class; decision of item 116). WHAT WAS BUILT, AND WHERE IT DIFFERS FROM ITEM 116: in `agents/llm/worker_base.py`, `_identity_in_text` takes its boundary from `_boundary(v)`, chosen by the shape of the normalised target value (new constants `_IPV4_LITERAL`, `_NAME_SHAPE`), and applies it to every reading of the text. IPv4 literal: not preceded by a digit or a dot; not followed by a digit, nor by a dot and a digit. Name (dot-separated labels of letters, digits, `_` and `-`, with at least one letter; this includes `ad_domain` values and single-label names): not preceded by a dot; not followed by `_` or `-`, nor by a dot and a digit (item 116 says the right side keeps the existing rule except letters and digits; the implementer read that as keeping the underscore and hyphen block on the right, so a hyphen or underscore on the right still stops a name and one on the left does not; the review layer may say if that was not meant). Everything else, that is an IPv6 literal, a cidr, a url, a repo, keeps the original rule, as item 116 asks for shapes the new rule does not hold for. ONE ADDITION BEYOND THE DESIGN, reported first: with the boundary rule alone 8 pre-registered cases stayed red, the hex byte `\x41` directly before an IPv4 lure (plain and nmap-escaped, both templates, both forms), because the hex pair's last digit is a digit glued to the address and no boundary on the address can tell that from a longer address; `_identity_in_text` therefore also searches the text with each `\xHH` (with any backslashes in front of it) turned into a space (new constant `_HEX_BYTE_ESCAPE`), each through the same three readings of `_text_forms`; it adds a reading and removes none. [2026-11-23 review layer, under the owner's standing delegation; the owner may veto] Record of the closure of S3 (items 143-146; nothing registered, nothing run but tests and read-only searches of the CLI's binary). B1, FOR TB-1'S NEW TABLE (reasoned from the code, not run): `_boundary` (`agents/llm/worker_base.py:380-405`) has three classes: an IPv4 literal (line 402: not after a digit or a dot, not before a digit or a dot and a digit), a name with a letter in it (line 404: not after a dot, not before `_`, `-` or a dot and a digit), and everything else, an IPv6 literal, a cidr, a url, a repo, by the original rule (line 405: not after a word character, a dot or a hyphen, not before a word character, a hyphen or a dot and a digit). `_text_forms` (lines 431-435) reads the text (1) with backslashes removed, (2) with backslashes as spaces, (3) with each control escape (`\n`, `\t`, `\r`, `\b`, `\f`, `\uXXXX` and the backslashes before it) as a space and the other backslashes removed. Forms found only by reading 2 or 3, through `_identity_in_text` (lines 325-354): for an IPv4 literal, a backslash between a digit and the address, `1\10.82.0.77` and `10.82.0.77\5` (reading 2 only: reading 1 glues the digit, reading 3 leaves `\1` alone and then removes the backslash), and a `\uXXXX` escape whose last digit is a digit directly before it, `\u000110.82.0.77` (reading 3 only); for a name, `x.\dc99.lure.example` and `dc99.lure.example\-x` (reading 2 only: reading 1 leaves a dot before or a hyphen after the name), and none found only by reading 3, since under this boundary a letter left by an escape never stops a name; for the original rule, a line break on either side, `10.82.0.0/24\n` (readings 2 and 3, not 1: the glued `n` is a word character) and `\n10.82.0.0/24` (reading 3 only: reading 2 leaves `n` before it), and the same for a url, an IPv6 literal or a repo. The mutant D69-TB-1 keeps only reading 1, so each of these forms is a counter-example; a form may also be found by the canonical-key path (`_text_identity_keys`, lines 586-594, which reads the same three forms token by token), which the table's first run will show. Reach: every target type of the schema (`TARGET_TYPES`, line 88: ip, cidr, fqdn, url, ad_domain, repo) reaches `_discovery_provenance` at line 849, inside `_to_proposal`, before the control plane; an unknown type is refused at lines 806-807. What the budget then does is after provenance: a cidr wider than /32 is denied as `target_count_exceeds_budget` (`control_plane/policy/rego/authz.rego:232-235`, with `max_targets` 1 at `control_plane/capability/broker.py:118`), so for an IPv4 range the escalation has no effect on the decision; a /32 cidr, an ip or a name can reach ALLOW, and for them it does. B2 AND TB-3: the first anchor of D69-TB-3 (`return any(re.search(pattern, form.lower()) for form in _text_forms(text))`) was replaced by lines 351-354 of `_identity_in_text` (`return any(re.search(pattern, form.lower()) for pattern in patterns for t in readings for form in _text_forms(t))`) when the hex-escape reading and the /32 pattern were added; the line is semantically the same (a match in any reading counts). The entry is re-anchored to it, with a comment saying so; the mutant is the same (for each pattern and reading, every reading of `_text_forms` must find the identity), and its second edit is unchanged; it is valid, 30 red. B3, THE NAME SCAN: the installed CLI (2.1.330) has 166 names that start `ANTHROPIC_`, `CLAUDE_CODE_`, `CLAUDE_`, `AWS_BEARER_`, `CLOUD_ML_` or `VERTEX_` and hold `TOKEN`, `KEY`, `AUTH`, `CRED`, `OAUTH`, `URL`, `ENDPOINT`, `BASE`, `PROVIDER` or `HOST`; 145 were in neither list. Step 1 facts: (a) the integrity guard of `scripts/run_shadow.py` asks `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all` (in `integrity()`), so an untracked file is refused; (b) `git check-ignore -v docs/D69_SHADOW_RECORDS/x.jsonl` matches no rule (exit 1; `.gitignore` ignores `evidence_store/` and `.mutation-journal.json`, no `*.jsonl`); (c) "the report's reading" (2g lines 397-398, 2f lines 357-358) is `scripts/plan_fit_report.collect(engagement_id)`, one engagement under row-level security, which takes an engagement id and not a record; a registered reading that wants the report's numbers would take the engagement ids from a record's banner line (its `engagements`), and would read the database, which `scripts/analyse_shadow.py` itself does not. The records directory: `REGISTERED_RECORDS_DIR` (still empty) is a path relative to the repository, as `REGISTERED_ACKS_DIR` (suggested `docs/D69_SHADOW_RECORDS`); an absolute path, one with `..`, one naming the repository itself or one that resolves outside it (a symbolic link) is `records_dir_outside_repository` at step 1; a path git ignores is `records_dir_ignored`, asked at step 2a with `git check-ignore` of a `*.jsonl` probe, the ledger, every record in the directory and the `--record` path (an `old-*.jsonl` rule, or `.git/info/exclude`, is seen; a tracked file is never reported); `--record` is still checked to be inside the directory and not to exist, and is refused under the ledger's name (`record_outside_registered_dir`). The history check, `record_history_problems(repo, records_dir, registered_commit, git)`: `git log --diff-filter=DMR --name-status -M -m --full-history --format="commit %h" <registered_commit>..HEAD -- <records_dir>` (`-M` reports a rename whatever `diff.renames` says, `-m` and `--full-history` what a merge brings in), `*.jsonl` only: D and R are `record_removed`, M is `record_modified`, the ledger's M is allowed and its D or R is `record_removed`; the line names the file and the short hash, never content; a git that raises, fails or prints a line that is not a status is `git_unavailable`. In the runner it is step 2a, after the integrity guard and before the factory string, the factory, the record's path checks and the acknowledgements: a question to git about the same checkout, refused before any role code is built, and before any record is read for an acknowledgement, whose sha256 is fixed only while no commit can change the record; no existing test's refusal code changed (the fake git of the guard's tests answers `check-ignore` with 1, nothing ignored). The acknowledgement check skips the ledger (`looks.jsonl` is not a record). The analysis, `python scripts/analyse_shadow.py look [--reason TEXT]`, with `REGISTERED_READING` (`"module:function"`, empty): (a) `run_shadow.registration_problems` (the runner's constants) and the reading (`reading_unregistered`), then `run_shadow.integrity` (the clean tree, untracked files included); (b) step 2a's two checks; (c) every `*.jsonl` but the ledger, by name, whose header names the registered commit, each with its sha256; a header that cannot be read or names no commit is `record_unreadable`; none is `no_records`; (d) the ledger: every line a look with `records`, numbered from 1, the last line ended (`ledger_corrupt`); appended to only (`ledger_rewritten`): HEAD's ledger is the start of the one on disk, and every commit since the registered one that changed it kept each parent's ledger as its start (the comparison with HEAD alone holds whenever the tree is clean, unless git is told to look away with `--assume-unchanged`, so each committed version is compared too); (e) a later look without `--reason` is `reason_required`, naming the number of looks and the first's time; (f) the reading is called with the records as read-only values (`ShadowRecord`: file, sha256, header, lines; mappings are `MappingProxyType`, lists tuples); an exception, or a result that is not one line of at most 200 characters, is `reading_failed` and nothing is written; then the line `{"look", "at", "records": [{"file", "sha256"}...], "verdict", "reason"}` is appended (flushed, fsynced) before the result is printed, so that a look is written down before its result is shown (2g's goal; a ledger that cannot be written is `ledger_unwritable` and shows nothing), and the print gives the records, a `NOTE` listing each record added, changed or missing since the previous look, and the verdict. Refusals are `refused: <code>: <explanation>` and exit 10. The script writes no database, imports no database or network client and runs no process but git. The mutation runner: `Mutation.retired` (not run, `RETIRED: <why>`, not a failure) and `Mutation.accepted_survivor` (run; INVALID is `ACCEPTED SURVIVOR: <why>`, not a failure; valid is `ACCEPTED SURVIVOR NOW VALID`, a failure); D69-SR-E8 is retired and D69-SL-2 an accepted survivor, their summaries kept. `scripts/run_shadow.main` takes `records_root` (what the records directory is relative to; the repository unless a test hands in its own), and the guard tests' autouse fixture hands it `tmp_path.parent`, `tmp_path.name` being the directory, so no test call site changed; two existing assertions changed with the rules they pin: `REASONS` has 29 codes (S6's four: `records_dir_outside_repository`, `records_dir_ignored`, `record_removed`, `record_modified`), and a relative records directory is no longer refused (the case became an absolute one, `records_dir_outside_repository`). Tests: 56 new (`tests/test_shadow_record_history.py` 24, `tests/test_shadow_ledger.py` 27, `tests/test_mutation_runner_states.py` 5), git real in a repository under `tmp_path`. Mutations, each red on an assertion: D69-SR-G1 (the reason check skipped with one look), G2 (the clean tree ignores untracked files), H1 (a rename ignored), H2 (a modification ignored), H3 (an ignored directory accepted), H4 (a failing log taken as no change), H5 (no `-M`), H6 (a directory resolving outside accepted), L1 (the ledger's committed versions not compared), L2 (its numbering not checked), L3 (the note leaves out an added record), L4 (a failed reading still writes the ledger), L5 (the ledger on disk not compared with HEAD's), L6 (the runner reads the ledger as a record), L7 (a record under the ledger's name), L8 (the reading given writable records); 16 valid; D69-SR-E2 and E12, whose anchors were kept, valid. Three-number check: local 7035 passed + 24 image errors = 7059 = 7003 + 56. Known limits: a look outside the analysis script (reading a record by hand, or `plan_fit_report.py` by hand) is not recorded, a rule for people (2g lines 409-410); the history is read from the registered commit on, so a record deleted before it is not seen; the ledger's protection is git's history itself, which a force push could rewrite, a matter for the repository's branch protection and not the code; a record changed on disk while git is told to look away (`--assume-unchanged`, `--skip-worktree`) is not refused, only noted by the next look as changed; the acknowledgement check reads `*.jsonl` directly in the directory, so a `--record` in a subdirectory or with another suffix, which step 7 still allows as before, is outside both the acknowledgements and the analysis (found by the implementer, not changed: not in this task's scope).

Measured: the clock source is `tsc`; 3000 consecutive transactions' `now()` had no equal and no backward pair, the smallest gap 0.000206 s. Re-run in the foreground: the test alone 20 times, 20 passed; its file 20 times, 20 passed; and it passed in this repair's full local run. The failure's message was not kept (only the run's last lines were read), so its root cause is not determined: neither the query's order nor two writes in one clock tick explains it, and what is left (a clock stepped back during the full run, or state another test left) is not shown. Not a cause shown here, but recorded: rows written in one transaction share `created_at` and then come back in the order of their random ids, not the order they were written, so "newest first" does not hold within a transaction; a caller that writes several evidence rows in one transaction would see them out of order. Suggested, not done: keep the full failure (`-ra` and the message) when it recurs; if order within a transaction matters, an ordering key that increases with each insert (an identity column), which is a schema change and needs its own authorization.

Found by the read-only investigations of 2026-11-27: on the inline ALLOW path the control plane does not compare a caller's `network_allowlist` with the authorized scope object, nor the scan target with the allowlist (`control_plane/api/function_api.py:519-1072`, `control_plane/orchestrator/dispatch.py:712`; no rule in `authz.rego`), so §8.3's egress boundary rests on each caller; the harness passes none, so an ip target gets its own `/32`, which fits no container (D62's measurement, `control_plane/scheduler/allowlist.py`), and an fqdn target makes `validate_allowlist` raise a plain `ValueError` that nothing catches, leaving the run `running` and the proposal `dispatching` (exit 12 in the harness). Decided: the inline path derives the allowlist from the authorized scope object with D62's `derive_network_allowlist`, and does not start on its skip reasons; a caller's allowlist must be CIDRs, inside the derived block, and hold the target; a refused start calls no sandbox and is recorded as not started; the sandbox's own allowlist refusal becomes a `NotStarted`. A proposal refused this way keeps the decision `ALLOW` (the policy allowed it), with no run, so a listed lure still triggers `lure_allowed`. The rules apply to NETWORK_ACTIONS only (the harness's whole action set), placed in `function_api._dispatch` so that the inline and the approved dispatch share one definition; web.* and ad.collect keep an unchecked caller allowlist, an open gap stated in the ADR; no new exception class or refusal code: `DockerSandbox.run` turns `validate_allowlist`'s `ValueError` into the base `NotStarted` (`sandbox_not_started`), because the set of `NotStarted` reasons is pinned to migration 0025's closed vocabulary (`tests/test_timeout_vs_not_started.py:104-108`, `tests/test_refusal_code.py:72-75`, `tests/test_harness_h5a1_result_summary.py:257-265`); the review layer's earlier decision of a new class was made without reading those pins and is withdrawn; `validate_allowlist` itself and its four assertions are unchanged; `tests/test_harness_observations_db.py` moves its ip scopes to the enclosing /24 with no assertion changed, because a /32 dispatch cannot start on a real sandbox (D62's measurement), so those tests exercised a path that cannot occur; the review layer's first draft applied R-a to every action and was stopped at A0-1 by the implementer's measurement of the whole suite, which found 9 test files whose dispatches it would have refused. Also recorded: both stops of this change (A0-1, and the new exception class) were made by the implementer on evidence; neither of the review layer's two original decisions had first checked the existing pins. Pre-registration: `tests/test_inline_dispatch_allowlist.py`, 195 lines, sha256 156134fb42a9119a7cd6a9273e34fb55c85206aa98db6b0124b7dfb94f000aed, twelve cases D1 to D12 (D12, `web.get`, added before any run, pins the rules' reach to NETWORK_ACTIONS) and the expectation before the repair (D2, D5 and D12 pass, the other nine fail; the unit test of R-d, outside the table, fails too), committed before its first run.

Found before S8's dry run (plan item 160 c) by the implementer, who ran the runner's three start checks in the clean environment before writing anything: the billing guard and the login passed (a subscription login), and the version check would refuse (`cli_version_unreviewed`: installed 2.1.332, reviewed 2.1.330), as runbook 3.6 (item 148) requires after an upgrade; nothing was written, so no registration, engagement or laboratory was left half made. Decided: the lists are reviewed again on 2.1.332 rather than the CLI pinned back (pinning changes the machine and may be undone by the CLI's own update); every name new in 2.1.332 is refused (`REVIEWED_REFUSE_3`), none is kept, so the lists only grow stricter; the login check's premises (the derivation of `authMethod` and the fields of `auth status`) are compared with item 145's text and are the same: the derivation in 2.1.332 (`N="none";if(H)N=M?"none":"third_party";else if(F==="claude.ai")N="claude.ai";...`) is item 145's text with its minified names changed (R to N, L to H, G to M, D to F, x to h, U to x, j to V), and is identical to it, character for character, once they are renamed back; the object `auth status` builds has the same fields in the same order (`loggedIn`, `authMethod`, `apiProvider`, `analyticsDisabled`, `projectsDirectory`, `configDirectory`, `loginExpiresAt`, then `forcedLoginMethod`, `gatewaySignInOffered`, `consoleProfileSignInOffered`, `allowedProviders`, `apiKeySource` only when an API key is available, and for `claude.ai` only `email`, `orgId`, `orgName`, `subscriptionType`, `storedClaudeAiLogin`); the 2.1.330 text kept in the earlier session ends at `storedClaudeAiLogin`, the `planTier` that follows it in 2.1.332 stands beside it in 2.1.330's own table of names, and it is not a field the check reads. The scan, with item 147's pattern (names that start `ANTHROPIC_`, `CLAUDE_CODE_`, `CLAUDE_`, `AWS_BEARER_`, `CLOUD_ML_` or `VERTEX_` and hold `TOKEN`, `KEY`, `AUTH`, `CRED`, `OAUTH`, `URL`, `ENDPOINT`, `BASE`, `PROVIDER` or `HOST`; item 143's first search is not written down as a pattern; its names that hold none of those words, the `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_*` switches, are outside this one and were checked by name, below): 168 names in 2.1.332, 163 already in a list and 5 new, `CLAUDE_CODE_HOST_SHORT_SCRATCHPAD`, `CLAUDE_CODE_HOST_STARTED_AHEAD`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USER_DELEGATED_SESSION_ROUTINE_SERVER_CHECK_PROVIDERS`, `CLAUDE_CODE_USER_DELEGATED_SESSION_SERVER_CHECK_PROVIDERS` and `CLAUDE_GATEWAY_IDP_CONNECT_BY_HOSTNAME`; the same search with a looser boundary (the name not required to stand alone) found eight more strings, read in their context: seven are not such a variable (the prefixes `AWS_BEARER_TOKEN_`, `CLAUDE_CODE_HOST_` and `CLAUDE_LOCAL_OAUTH_` of listed names; the code constants `ANSWER_ON_CLAUDE_AI_URL`, `PLATFORM_CLAUDE_HOST` and `DEFAULT_VERTEX_OPUS_KEY`; and `ANT_CLAUDE_CODE_METRICS_ENDPOINT`, a telemetry variable whose prefix is outside the pattern, not added), and one is, `_CLAUDE_CODE_ASSUME_FIRST_PARTY_BASE_URL` ("... is already set"), a provider base url behind a leading underscore, refused as the sixth name of `REVIEWED_REFUSE_3`; every name of the lists (all 174, inside the pattern or not) is still in the 2.1.332 binary, so no name reviewed on 2.1.330 is missing (none is removed from the lists); item 147 counted 166 names in 2.1.330 while 163 of the listed names fall in the pattern now, a difference of three that the record does not explain, because item 147's exact extraction was not written down. In a clean environment `claude auth status --json` (no model call) gave the fields `analyticsDisabled`, `apiProvider`, `authMethod`, `configDirectory`, `consoleProfileSignInOffered`, `loggedIn`, `loginExpiresAt` and `projectsDirectory`, `authMethod` `oauth_token`, `apiProvider` `firstParty`. `claude --version` prints `2.1.332 (Claude Code)` and a newline, which `query_cli_version` reads as 2.1.332. `REVIEWED_CLI_VERSION` is "2.1.332". Tests: the names written out in `tests/test_shadow_runner_names_b3.py` (`V332_REFUSE`, two new tests, one of them that the lists cover the 2.1.332 scan) and `tests/test_shadow_runner_auth.py`'s `EXPECTED` (six more cases of the per-name refusal); and, because the version is pinned in tests, the literal "2.1.330" becomes "2.1.332" in `tests/test_shadow_runner_cli_version.py` (five lines), `tests/test_shadow_runner_record.py:119` and `tests/test_shadow_runner_guard.py:82`, outside the task's listed files, made by the implementer and held for the review layer's authorization before the commit. A later upgrade will refuse again; that is the guard working, and auto-update is not turned off, since that would change the machine's configuration.
